[Basílica i Temple Expiatori de la Sagrada Família](59545.md), the tallest church building in the world. This photo was taken on 8 March 2026, sixteen days after it reached its final height.

This **list of tallest church buildings** ranks [church buildings](6325.md) by height. From the [Middle Ages](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Middle_Ages) until the advent of the [skyscraper](29485.md), [Christian church](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christian_church) buildings were often the [world's tallest buildings](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_the_world's_tallest_buildings). From 1311, when the [spire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spire) of [Lincoln Cathedral](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lincoln_Cathedral) surpassed the height of the [Great Pyramid of Giza](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_Pyramid_of_Giza), until the [Washington Monument](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Washington_Monument) was completed in 1884, a succession of church buildings held this title.

- The tallest church building in the world is the [Sagrada Família](59545.md) in Barcelona, which surpassed [Ulm Minster](853115.md) (161.53 m) on 30 October 2025 when its central tower reached 162.91 m.

  [^cite_note-SF2025-1]

  &#x20;The central tower reached the final height of 172.5 m on 20 February 2026.

  [^cite_note-2]

- The tallest completed church building in the world was formerly the [Ulm Minster](853115.md) (161.53 m),

  [^cite_note-\:12-3]

  &#x20;the main [Lutheran](23371382.md) congregation in [Ulm](189455.md), [Germany](11867.md).

- The tallest [domed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dome) church building, and the tallest [Catholic](606848.md) church until surpassed by Sagrada Família, is the [Basilica of Our Lady of Peace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Basilica_of_Our_Lady_of_Peace_of_Yamoussoukro) (158 m)

  [^cite_note-\:16-4]

  &#x20;in [Yamoussoukro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yamoussoukro), [Ivory Coast](57744.md).

- The tallest [cathedral](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cathedral) as well as the tallest church building with two [steeples](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Steeple) is [Cologne Cathedral](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cologne_Cathedral) (157.22 m)

  [^cite_note-\:13-5]

  &#x20;in [Cologne](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cologne), Germany.

- The tallest domed cathedral as well as the tallest [Eastern Orthodox](10186.md) is [People's Salvation Cathedral](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/People's_Salvation_Cathedral) (132 m)

  [^cite_note-\:11-6]

  &#x20;in [Bucharest](36877.md), [Romania](25445.md).

  [^cite_note-\:0-7]

- The tallest [brickwork](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brickwork) church building is [St Martin's Church](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/St._Martin's_Church,_Landshut) (130.6 m) in [Landshut](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Landshut), Germany.

- The tallest brickwork church building with two steeples is [St Mary's Church](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/St._Mary's_Church,_L%C3%BCbeck) (125 m) in [Lübeck](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/L%C3%BCbeck), Germany.

- The tallest wooden church building is [Săpânța-Peri Monastery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S%C4%83p%C3%A2n%C8%9Ba-Peri_Monastery) church (78 m) in [Săpânța](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S%C4%83p%C3%A2n%C8%9Ba), Romania.

  [^cite_note-\:14-8]

- The tallest church building in the [Americas](29833.md) is the [Cathedral of Maringá](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cathedral_of_Maring%C3%A1) (124 m) in [Maringá](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maring%C3%A1), [Brazil](3383.md).

The cities with the most churches surpassing 100 metres (330 feet) are [Hamburg](13467.md) (5 of the 28[^cite_note-9] tallest churches, with 5 towers overall) and [Lübeck](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/L%C3%BCbeck) (4 of the 55[^cite_note-10] tallest churches, two of which with twin towers → 6 towers overall), followed by [Tallinn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tallinn) (2), [St. Petersburg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/St._Petersburg) (2), [New York City](645042.md) (2), [Dortmund](149349.md) (2) and [Stralsund](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stralsund) (2).

The cities with the most churches surpassing 75 metres (246 feet) are [Berlin](3354.md) (16), [Hamburg](13467.md) (9), [Paris](22989.md) (8), [Dresden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dresden) (8), [Vienna](55866.md) (7), [Stockholm](26741.md) (7) and [Munich](19058.md) (7), while in the Americas it is [New York City](645042.md) (4).

| 1240–1311 | [Old St Paul's Cathedral](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Old_St_Paul's_Cathedral) |  | 149.0 m (489.0 ft) | 13.7% | 71 years | 111.3 m (365.0 ft) (new [St Paul's Cathedral](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/St_Paul's_Cathedral)) | [London](17867.md) | The cathedral stood on the site of the present [St Paul's Cathedral](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/St_Paul's_Cathedral) until 1666. | 1311–1549 | [Lincoln Cathedral](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lincoln_Cathedral) |  | 160.0 m (524.9 ft) | 7.4% | 238 years | 83.0 m (272.3 ft) | [Lincoln](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lincoln,_England) | Tallest ever building until 1890, only then was its original height surpassed by Ulm Minster; central spire collapsed in 1549 |
| 1549–1569 | [St. Mary's Church](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/St._Mary's_Church,_Stralsund) |  | 151.0 m (495.4 ft) | −5.4% | 20 years (94 years) | 104.0 m (341.2 ft) | [Stralsund](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stralsund) | First mentioned in 1298; collapse of first tower in 1382; second, 151 metres (495 feet) tall spire finished in 1485 |
| 1569–1573 | [Beauvais Cathedral](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beauvais_Cathedral) |  | 153.0 m (502.0 ft) | 1.3% | 4 years | 47.5 m (155.8 ft) | [Beauvais](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beauvais) | Tower collapsed in 1573 |
| 1573–1647 | [St. Mary's Church](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/St._Mary's_Church,_Stralsund) |  | 151.0 m (495.4 ft) | −1.3% | 74 years (94 years) | 104.0 m (341.2 ft) | [Stralsund](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stralsund) | Second spire burned down after a lightning strike in 1647; current, third spire finished in 1708 |
| 1647–1874 | [Strasbourg Cathedral](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Strasbourg_Cathedral) |  | 142.0 m (465.9 ft) | −6% | 227 years | 142.0 m (465.9 ft) | [Strasbourg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Strasbourg) | Finished in 1439; tallest structure built and finished in the Middle Ages; earliest church to have been the world's tallest which still stands at its original height |
| 1874–1876 | [St. Nicholas Church](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/St._Nicholas_Church,_Hamburg) |  | 147.3 m (483.3 ft) | 3.5% | 2 years | 147.3 m (483.3 ft) | [Hamburg](13467.md) | Bombed in 1943; demolition of most of the church in 1951; tower remains and serves as a war memorial |
| 1876–1880 | [Rouen Cathedral](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rouen_Cathedral) |  | 151.0 m (495.4 ft) | 2.7% | 4 years | 151.0 m (495.4 ft) | [Rouen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rouen) | Church finished in 1506; its cast iron spire was built between 1825 and 1876; [painted](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rouen_Cathedral_\(Monet_series\)) by Claude Monet |
| 1880–1890 | [Cologne Cathedral](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cologne_Cathedral) |  | 157.4 m (516.4 ft) | 4.2% | 10 years | 157.4 m (516.4 ft) | [Cologne](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cologne) | Only church with two main towers to ever have been the world's tallest |
| 1890–2025 | [Ulm Minster](853115.md) |  | 161.5 m (529.9 ft) | 2.6% | 135 years | 161.5 m (529.9 ft) | [Ulm](189455.md) | First time since 1311 that a church surpassed the original height of Lincoln Cathedral; intentionally built a few metres taller than Cologne Cathedral |
| since 2025 | [Sagrada Família](59545.md) |  | 172.5 m (565.9 ft) | 6.9% |  | 172.5 m (565.9 ft) | [Barcelona](4443.md) | Construction began in 1882. Tallest church of all time. |

## Constructed

### Church buildings ≥ 100 m (328 ft)

This list does not include church buildings that incorporate a significant portion of space to non-church uses, such as the [Chicago Temple Building](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago_Temple_Building). It does not include structures from non-Christian religions.

### Church buildings ≥ 75 m (246 ft) < 95 m (325 ft)

**Note:** The church buildings are ordered based on their tallest recorded height in history. Those listed in *italics* and marked with *H* (for "historical") are church buildings no longer in existence (suffix *D*) or no longer as tall as their previous maximum height (suffix *>99* if height today > 99 metres, suffix *>75* if height today > 75 metres and suffix *<75* if height today below 75 metres). These church buildings may appear a second time on the list if their current shorter height is still greater than 75 metres (3,000 inches). In order to view the tallest *present* churches in a row click the sorting button in the *H* column.

## Auxiliary list

This list completely follows the pattern of the main list (≥ 99 m). Church buildings are placed here that have been removed from the main list because the data about them is disputed or contradictory, or there is no information about the respective church building anywhere on the Internet. The purpose of this list is to preserve the work and efforts of the authors who have included some church buildings with a disputed place in the main list. In this way their work is preserved, and if it is proved that the excluded church buildings have a place in the main list, they can easily be restored to it (since the present list follows the pattern of the main list completely).
